ii ် ֻ


ٝݓ॓࠯ն࿐࿹࣮ളჽѰൖ࿐໊ં໓
Abstract
At present, in the global “Industrial 4.0” revolutionary process and “Made in China
2025” strategic plan, robots as the core technology receives unprecedented attention. Take
the United States as an example, unmanned car, multiple humanoid robots designed by
Google, Amazon warehouse guided vehicle, and NASA Curiosity Mars vehicle attract
worldwide media attention. Europe, Japan and China have also launched their own robotic
research programs.
Facing the complex and volatile security requirements and the harsh and uncertain
external environment of the robot system and its swarm tasks, how to safely control each
distributed robot node to complete tasks in the external environment is a serious and urgent
challenge. Traditionally, designers control a multirobot system by implementing and
deploying their controller to the system. This process is cumbersome and errorprone.
The controller designed is also extremely difficult to ensure that the system can safely
and stably complete a given task. With the breakthroughs in the theory and technology of
formal methods in recent years, the verification of safety quality of robotic systems based
on formal methods has been deeply studied by many researchers.
In order to systematically verify and guarantee the security of the robot and its swarm
system, runtime monitoring based on formal methods is a necessary and effective means,
which can effectively improve the system reliability, and it is also a research hot spot
at present. Among many monitoring methods for hardware and software systems, Run
time Verification (RV) is a lightweight software reliability assurance technology that has
emerged in recent years. It determines whether a given property is satisfied or not based
on the operating trajectory of the target system. Because of the uniqueness of the sys
tem running track, it effectively overcomes the problem of state space explosion of model
checking technology and the difficulty of covering the full path of test when the system is
sufficiently complex.In addition, unlike traditional software reliability assurance technol
ogy, RV technology is mainly used after system deployment, which can monitor system
defects caused by uncertain operating environment and context. These characteristics
make RV technology suitable for application on CPS system of robot swarm. However,
due to the complexity of the robot system and the high learning threshold of formal theory
in RV technology. At present, the research on RV technology in the robot swarm system
ֻ iii ်


ٝݓ॓࠯ն࿐࿹࣮ളჽѰൖ࿐໊ં໓
is still at an early stage.
Therefore, this paper mainly studies how to apply the runtime verification method
to a robot system with uncertain characteristics. On this basis, it also studies the methods
of compulsory protection against the runtime property violation of the robot system. The
innovations of our work include:
1) By analyzing the characteristics and security challenges of robots based on ROS
(Robot Operating System), this paper proposes a runtime verification framework RMoM
for ROSbased robots and their swarm systems, aiming at ensuring autonomous behaviors
such as robot navigation, obstacle avoidance, and swarm cooperative control always meet
the security protocol in the running state. Through the study of RV theory of robot CPS
system, the monitoring methods for robot and swarm systems based on ROS are further
enriched.
2) Current RV methods for robotic systems do not take into account the monitoring of
hybrid property and swarm property. In this paper, On the basis of temporal logic language
MTL (Metric Temporal Logic), a threevalue semantics extension is made for limited
sequence and predictive monitoring requirements. Swarm propositions and operators are
added in the temporal logic language MTL
3
RMoM which is more adaptable in the field of
robot swarm. Based on this logic language, the swarm property and time interval property
in the robot swarm system can be expressed and monitored.
3) The application of RV technology in ROS swarm needs to be based on global
and local constraints of the robot system. It involves various events and states. This
method achieves a customized and highly scalable monitoring interface library of ROS
robot swarm system. Based on RMoM framework, a kind of monitoring interface library
for robot swarm is presented. Monitoring method for hierarchical properties, which can
now cover swarm properties, singlerobot properties, hardware resources and communi
cation layer properties for runtime verification.
4) We have also studied the protection methods against the violation of the robot
system properties, proposed a macromicro runtime enforcement mechanism and inno
vatively proposed a Dtime enforcement generator. This enforcement works flexibly in
periodic time steps and does not require knowledge of the state of the next runtime envi
ronment. This runtime enforcement generator algorithm is implemented and deployed in
a robotflocksim simulation tool for robot swarms. The safety and security improvements
and overhead caused by this method are experimentally tested.
iv ် ֻ


ٝݓ॓࠯ն࿐࿹࣮ളჽѰൖ࿐໊ં໓
Key Words: Robot System, Runtime Verification, Hierarchical Property,
Temporal Logic, Uncertain Environment, Distributed System, Runtime En
forcement

// BNF below is the JavaMOP specification syntax
<JavaMOP Specification> ::= {<Modifier>} <Id> [“(” <Java Parameters> “)”] “{”
<Java Declarations>
{<Event>}
{<Property>
{“@” <LOGIC State> “{” <Java Statements> “}”}}
“}”
<Modifier> ::= “unsynchronized” | “decentralized” | “perthread” | “suffix”
<Event> ::= [“creation”] “event” <Id> <AspectJ advice> “{” [ <Java State
ments>] “}”
<Property> ::= <LOGIC Name> “:” <LOGIC Syntax>
<Java Declarations> ::= ... <! syntax of declarations in Java >
<Java Parameters> ::= ... <! syntax of method parameter list in Java >
<Java Statements> ::= ... <! slightly extended syntax of statements in Java >
<AspectJ Advice> ::= ... <! slightly extended syntax of advice in AspectJ >
